manifolds and then coning off the boundary. The smooth manifold with cone
attached to the boundary then possesses a smooth stratification such that a
Goresky-triangulation may be taken to be the Whitehead triangulation, coned off.
The map is injective as is seen by carefully verifying that Siegel’s
singular surgeries can be carried out within the class of smoothly stratified spaces.
In addition to the fact that Siegel’s intersection form invariant

w(-)\in\mathrm{Witt}(\mathbb{Q})
is a
topological
invariant,
one exploits the observation that if the triangulation restricts to a
smooth embedding on the interiors of simplices, then the skeletal
filtration determined by the triangulation is indeed a smooth Thom-Mather
stratification.)
Finally,
t
is an equivalence, for
t
is a natural transformation of
homology theories which is an isomorphism on coefficient groups.
∎

5.
Analytic transfer of the signature orientation along a fibration

The goal of this section is to describe how to assign to every fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces

W-X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y,

in which the fibers are oriented Witt pseudomanifolds, a class in the KK-group
\mathrm{KK}(C_{0}(X),C_{0}(Y)).

Indeed from
[
ALMP12
,
AGR23
]
the family of vertical signature operators associated to any choice of vertical wedge metric is a family
of Fredholm operators and we will see that it defines the desired class. To do this, in order to apply the theory developed in
[
AGR23
]
, we discuss the “grid resolution” of this fiber bundle to a fiber bundle
of manifolds with corners with iterated fibration structures,

\mathrm{res}(W)-\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)\longrightarrow\mathrm{res}(Y).

5.1.
Resolutions of a stratified fiber bundle

Starting with a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces

W-X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y,

we will discuss the partial resolutions that result from resolving
the base or the fibers and the grid resolution that results from resolving both.
The grid resolution continues to participate in a fiber bundle. One could go further and fully resolve
X
but,
unless one of
W
or
Y
is smooth, at the cost of the fiber bundle structure.

In the simplest interesting situation when
X
is the product of two stratified spaces of positive depth,
X=W\times Y,
endowed with the projection
onto
Y,

p:X\longrightarrow Y,
the partial resolutions of base and fiber are, respectively

\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)=W\times\mathrm{res}(Y),\quad\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)=\mathrm{res}(W)\times Y,

and the grid resolution of
X
is

\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X,p)=\mathrm{res}(W)\times\mathrm{res}(Y).

This is a smooth manifold with corners but, as neither
Y
nor
W
are smooth, this is not equal to the resolution of
X
and
it does not have a natural iterated fibration structure. Indeed in
[
KR23
]
Kottke and Rochon have shown that

\mathrm{res}(X)
can be obtained from
\mathrm{res}(W)\times\mathrm{res}(Y)
by a sequence of real blow-ups of
appropriate submanifolds of the boundary producing what they call the ‘ordered product’
\widetilde{\times}
so that

\mathrm{res}(X)=\mathrm{res}(W)\widetilde{\times}\mathrm{res}(Y).

It is worth noting that the lifted projection
\mathrm{res}(X)\longrightarrow\mathrm{res}(Y)
is no longer a fiber bundle map
(it is a “
b
-fibration”), as this is one of the reasons why we have use for the grid resolution.

While the grid resolution is a smooth manifold with corners, the partial resolutions of base and fiber above are the product of a smooth manifold with corners and a smoothly stratified space. For the purpose of regularity we consider such a product as a smoothly stratified space with the stratification obtained by endowing the manifold with corners with its natural stratification (see Remark
2.7
).

Prelude: When the base and fiber each have depth one.

Since the constructions below are a bit intricate, we thought it would be useful to first describe the simplest interesting situation. This prelude can be skipped.

Suppose that
W\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
is a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces where
W
has singular part
W_{0}
and regular part
W_{1}
and
Y
has singular part
Y_{0}
and regular part
Y_{1}
and
X
has poset stratification given by the product poset

\textstyle{(0,1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(0,0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(1,1)}
\textstyle{(1,0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where we have chosen that the first entry corresponds to the fiber and the second entry corresponds to the base.
The regular part of
X
is
X_{\mathrm{reg}}=X_{(1,1)},
the singular part of
X
is
X_{\mathrm{sing}}=X_{(0,0)}\cup X_{(0,1)}\cup X_{(1,0)}.
The singular stata
X_{(0,1)}
and
X_{(1,0)}
are disjoint but their closures are both obtained by the inclusion of
X_{(0,0)}.
Note that
p^{-1}(Y_{0})=X_{(0,0)}\cup X_{(1,0)}
and
p^{-1}(Y_{1})=X_{(0,1)}\cup X_{(1,1)}.

Resolving along the base:

The resolution of the base
\mathrm{res}(Y)
comes with a blow-down map
\beta_{Y}:\mathrm{res}(Y)\longrightarrow Y
and we obtain the partial resolution of
X
along the base by pulling-back
p
along
\beta_{Y},
i.e.,

\textstyle{\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{p}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{Y}}
\textstyle{Y.}

The induced map
\bar{p}
participates in a fiber bundle

W\;\operatorname{---}\;\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\overline{p}\phantom{x}}\mathrm{res}(Y)

(and for the purposes of regularity we note that if we regard
\mathrm{res}(Y)
with its natural stratification as a manifold with boundary, i.e., the stratification with strata
\partial\mathrm{res}(Y)=\partial_{0}\mathrm{res}(Y)
and
\mathrm{res}(Y)^{\circ},
then
\bar{p}
is a smooth fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces).
The new base
\mathrm{res}(Y)
is a manifold with fibered boundary with boundary fiber bundle

F_{0}\mathrm{res}(Y)\;\operatorname{---}\;\partial_{0}\mathrm{res}(Y)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\phi_{0}\phantom{x}}B_{0}\mathrm{res}(Y)=Y_{0}.

We denote the pre-image of the boundary of
\mathrm{res}(Y)
under
\bar{p}
by
\partial_{0}^{h}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)
with the
h
exponent denoting ‘horizontal’, it fits into the restriction of the pull-back diagram above,

\textstyle{\partial_{0}^{h}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{p}}
\textstyle{X_{(0,0)}\cup X_{(1,0)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\partial_{0}\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{0}}
\textstyle{Y_{0}}

since
\phi_{0}=\beta_{Y}|_{\partial_{0}\mathrm{res}(Y)}.
The unlabeled arrow in this diagram is a fiber bundle map, just as the labeled arrows are, and we denote it by

F_{0}^{h}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)\;\operatorname{---}\;\partial_{0}^{h}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\phi_{0}^{h}\phantom{x}}X_{(0,0)}\cup X_{(1,0)}=:B_{0}^{h}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p).

Note that here
F_{0}^{h}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)=F_{0}\mathrm{res}(Y)
is a smooth manifold and
B_{0}^{h}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)
is a smoothly stratified space. We refer to the fiber bundle
\phi_{0}^{h}
and its compatibility with the fiber bundle
\phi_{0}
as a
horizontal iterated fibration structure
on
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p).

Resolving along the fibers:

Returning to our fiber bundle
W\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y,
we can use the fact that
p
is a smooth map of smoothly stratified spaces to see that it induces a fiber bundle

W_{0}\;\operatorname{---}\;X_{(0,0)}\cup X_{(0,1)}\longrightarrow Y.

We can use this to simultaneously resolve all of the fibers of
p
to obtain the partial resolution of
X
with respect to the fibers,
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p).
The resolution maintains the fiber bundle structure and we denote the result by

\mathrm{res}(W)\;\operatorname{---}\;\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\widehat{p}\phantom{x}}Y.

This partial resolution replaced
X_{(0,0)}\cup X_{(0,1)}
with a new boundary face which we denote
\partial_{0}^{v}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}X,
with the
v
exponent denoting ‘vertical’.
The restriction of
\widehat{p}
to this face is a fiber bundle over
Y
with fiber the boundary of the resolution of
W,

\partial_{0}\mathrm{res}(W)\;\operatorname{---}\;\partial_{0}^{v}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\widehat{p}\phantom{x}}Y.

The blow-down maps of the fibers,
\beta_{W}:\mathrm{res}(W)\longrightarrow W,
fit together into
\beta_{\mathrm{fib}}:\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\longrightarrow X
so that

\textstyle{\mathrm{res}(W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{W}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{p}}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{\mathrm{fib}}}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}

and if we restrict this diagram to
\partial_{0}^{v}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p),
we get

\textstyle{\partial_{0}\mathrm{res}(W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{0}=\beta_{W}|_{\partial_{0}\mathrm{res}(W)}}
\textstyle{\partial_{0}^{v}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{0}^{v}=\beta_{\mathrm{fib}}|_{\partial_{0}^{v}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)}}
\scriptstyle{\hat{p}}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{W_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{(0,0)}\cup X_{(0,1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}

This shows how the boundary fiber bundles of the fibers of
\widehat{p},

F_{0}\mathrm{res}(W)\;\operatorname{---}\;\partial_{0}\mathrm{res}(W)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\phi_{0}\phantom{x}}B_{0}\mathrm{res}(W)=W_{0},

fit together into

F_{0}^{v}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\;\operatorname{---}\;\partial_{0}^{v}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\phi_{0}^{v}\phantom{x}}B_{0}^{v}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)=X_{(0,0)}\cup X_{(0,1)}.

We refer to the fiber bundle
\phi_{0}^{v}
and its compatibility with the fiber bundles
\phi_{0}
and
\widehat{p}
as a
vertical iterated fibration structure
on
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p).

Resolving both the base and the fibers:
If we resolve the fiber bundle
W\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
along the base we again have a stratified fiber bundle whose fiber is
W
and so we may proceed as above and resolve along the fibers. We refer to the result as the grid resolution of
X,

\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p),\overline{p}).

The result is a smooth manifold with corners that participates in a smooth fiber bundle

\mathrm{res}(W)\;\operatorname{---}\;\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\widetilde{p}\phantom{x}}\mathrm{res}(Y)

where the base and the fiber are smooth manifolds with fibered boundary. The only displeasing aspect of
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)
is that it does not have a natural structure of manifold with fibered corners. The problem is that the two boundary hypersurfaces of
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)
correspond to the labels
(0,1)
and
(1,0)
and, since these are non-comparable elements of the poset, their corresponding boundary hypersurfaces should not intersect. This is easily fixed, we need only blow-up their intersection to obtain the resolution of
X,

\mathrm{res}(X)=[\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X);\partial_{0}^{v}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)\cap\partial_{0}^{h}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)].

As this blow-up does not treat all of the fibers of
\widetilde{p}
equally, the fiber bundle structure of
p
does not survive beyond the grid resolution.

Returning to the general case, let
W\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
be a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces, so that the poset of
X
satisfies

S(X)=S(W)\times S(Y)

(with the obvious notation). By resolving the strata corresponding to
(\max S(W))\times S(Y)
of
X
we will obtain the partial base resolution
of
X,
which will fiber over the resolution of
Y
with typical fiber
W,
and similarly by resolving the strata corresponding to

S(W)\times(\max S(Y))
of
X
we will obtain the partial fiber resolution of
X,
which will fiber over
Y
with typical fiber the resolution of
W.

In general neither of these partial resolutions will result in a smooth manifold with corners. Once we perform both resolutions (in either order) we will
obtain the grid resolution of
X,

\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X),
which will be a smooth manifold with corners but it will not have a natural iterated
fibration structure. Instead it will have two structures, one inherited from the base
Y
and the other inherited from the fiber
W.
We will start by defining
these structures, then we will construct the grid resolution, and then we will show that it has the advertised properties.

Definition 5.1
.

(1)

Let
W\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\psi\phantom{x}}N
be a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces in which the base is a smooth manifold with corners.
A
horizontal iterated fibration structure
consists of an iterated fibration structure on the base
N,
associated to the boundary
stratification
N\longrightarrow S(N),
such that:

•

For all
\alpha\in S(N),
with corresponding fiber bundle
F_{\alpha}N\;\operatorname{---}\;\partial_{\alpha}N\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\phi_{\alpha}\phantom{x}}B_{\alpha}N,

\partial_{\alpha}^{h}X:=\psi^{-1}(\partial_{\alpha}N)

is either a disjoint union of connected codimension one strata of
X
or the regular part of
X,

and participates in a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces

F_{\alpha}^{h}X\;\operatorname{---}\;\partial_{\alpha}^{h}X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\phi_{\alpha}^{h}\phantom{x}}B_{a}^{h}X.

If
\alpha,\gamma\in S(N)
are such that
\partial_{\alpha}^{h}X\cap\partial_{\gamma}^{h}X\neq\emptyset
and
\alpha<\gamma,

there is a commutative diagram of fiber bundles of smoothly stratified spaces

\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}^{h}X\cap\partial_{\gamma}^{h}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\gamma}^{h}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}^{h}}
\textstyle{\partial_{\gamma}^{h}B_{\alpha}^{h}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\gamma\alpha}^{h}}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}^{h}X}

where
\partial_{\gamma}^{h}B_{\alpha}^{h}X
is a disjoint union of connected codimension one strata of
B_{\alpha}^{h}X.

•

For all
\alpha\in S(N),
the restriction of
\psi
to a map
\partial_{\alpha}^{h}X\longrightarrow\partial_{\alpha}N
is the pull-back of a fiber bundle

B_{\alpha}^{h}X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\psi_{\alpha}^{h}\phantom{x}}B_{\alpha}N,
i.e.,

(5.1)

\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}^{h}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}^{h}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}^{h}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\alpha}^{h}}
\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}N}

is a pull-back diagram.

(2)

Let
L\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\psi\phantom{x}}Y
be a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces in which the fiber is a smooth manifold with corners.
A
vertical iterated fibration structure
12
12

12

When the base is smooth, this is referred to as a
locally trivial family of manifolds with corners and iterated fibration structures
in
[
AGR23
, Definition 1.3]
.
consists of an iterated fibration structure on the fiber
L,
associated to the boundary
stratification
L\longrightarrow S(L),
such that:

•

For all
a\in S(L),
with corresponding fiber bundle
F_{a}L\;\operatorname{---}\;\partial_{a}L\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\phi_{a}\phantom{x}}B_{a}L,
there is
\partial_{a}^{v}X\subseteq X,
either a disjoint
union of connected codimension one strata of
X
or the regular part of
X,
such that
\psi
restricted to
\partial_{a}^{v}X
fibers over
Y
with fiber

\partial_{a}L.
Each
\partial_{a}^{v}X
participates in a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces

F_{a}^{v}X\;\operatorname{---}\;\partial_{a}^{v}X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\phi_{a}^{v}\phantom{x}}B_{a}^{v}X.

If
a,b\in S(L)
are such that
\partial_{a}^{v}X\cap\partial_{b}^{v}X\neq\emptyset
and
a<b,
there is a commutative diagram of fiber bundles of smoothly
stratified spaces

\textstyle{\partial_{a}^{v}X\cap\partial_{b}^{v}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{b}^{v}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{a}^{v}}
\textstyle{\partial_{b}^{v}B_{a}^{v}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{ba}^{v}}
\textstyle{B_{a}^{v}X}

where
\partial_{b}^{v}B_{a}^{v}X
is a disjoint union of connected codimension one strata of
B_{a}^{v}X.

•

For all
a\in S(L),
there is a fiber bundle map
B_{a}L\;\operatorname{---}\;B_{a}^{v}X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\psi_{a}^{v}\phantom{x}}Y
participating in the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\partial_{a}^{v}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{v}_{a}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{B_{a}^{v}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{a}^{v}}
\textstyle{Y}

(3)

We say that a fiber bundle carries a
grid iterated fibration structure
if it is equipped with both a horizontal and a vertical iterated fibration structure.

Starting with a fiber bundle
W\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
of smoothly stratified spaces, we obtain the partial resolution corresponding to the base by
considering the pull-back of
p
along the blow-down map from the resolution of
Y,

(5.2)

\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p):=\beta_{Y}^{*}X,\quad\text{ where }\quad\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern The partial resolution corresponding to the fiber is obtained by resolving the strata of
X
corresponding to
S(W)\times(\max S(Y)).
As
these strata are transverse to the fibers of
p,
the composition of the blow-down map with
p
is again a fiber bundle map,
\widehat{p},

\textstyle{\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\widehat{p}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{Y}

with typical fiber
\mathrm{res}(W).

Indeed, given an open cover of
Y,

\{\cal U_{\ell}\}
that is trivializing for
p
and the corresponding transition functions

\phi_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:\cal U_{\ell}\cap\cal U_{\ell^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Diff}(W)
(valued in the stratified diffeomorphisms of
W
), we can lift these to

\widetilde{\phi}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:\cal U_{\ell}\cap\cal U_{\ell^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Diff}(\mathrm{res}(W))
(valued in the fibered diffeomorphisms of
\mathrm{res}(W)
)
and obtain transition functions for
\widehat{p}.

Finally the grid resolution of
W\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
is obtained by performing both of these partial resolutions,

\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X,p)=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p),\bar{p})

and so fits into the diagram

(5.3)

\textstyle{\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X,p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\beta}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{p}}
\textstyle{\beta_{Y}^{*}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{p}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{Y}}
\textstyle{Y.}

Theorem 5.2
.

If
W\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
is a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces then its partial resolutions corresponding to the base and fiber inherit
a horizontal and vertical iterated fibration structure, respectively. Its grid resolution thus has a grid iteration fibration structure.

Proof.

To simplify notation we assume during this proof that two strata are comparable if and only if their closures intersect. Thus, e.g., the closure of
W_{a}
is
W_{\leq a}.

Let us start by establishing the vertical iterated fibration structure of the partial resolution corresponding to the fiber.

Without loss of generality let us assume that
Y
is connected and let us denote by
\circ
the maximum on
S(Y),
so that
Y_{\circ}

is equal to the regular part of
Y.

Every stratum
W_{a},

a\in S(W),
corresponds to a stratum
X_{a\times\circ},
where
a\times\circ\in S(W)\times S(Y)=S(X),

and by restricting
p
we obtain a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces

W_{\leq a}\;\operatorname{---}\;Z_{\leq a}:=X_{(\leq a)\times(\leq\circ)}\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p_{\leq a}:=p|_{X_{(\leq a)\times(\leq\circ)}}\phantom{x}}Y.

Just as discussed above for
p,

p_{\leq a}
lifts to the resolution of
Z_{\leq a}
corresponding to the fibers,

\widehat{p}_{\leq a}:\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(Z_{\leq a},p_{\leq a})\longrightarrow Y.

Now if we recall from Theorem
2.10
(see (
2.6
)) that
\mathrm{res}(W_{\leq a})
is the base of the boundary fiber bundle of
W
corresponding to
a,

\partial_{a}\mathrm{res}(W)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\phi_{a}\phantom{x}}B_{a}\mathrm{res}(W),
then since resolving the stratum
X_{a\times\circ}
of
X
resolves the corresponding stratum
of each fiber, we see that we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\partial_{a}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{a}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{p}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{res}(Z_{\leq a})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{p}_{\leq a}}
\textstyle{Y}

and hence the vertical iterated fibration structure we expected.

Next let us establish the horizontal iterated fibration structure of the partial resolution corresponding to the base.
Let us momentarily introduce the notation

\widetilde{X}=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p).

For each
\alpha\in S(Y)
the stratum
Y_{\alpha}
of
Y
has closure
Y_{\leq\alpha}
and we denote the restriction of
p
to this closure by

\textstyle{X|_{\leq\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p|_{\leq\alpha}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{Y_{\leq\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y}

where
X|_{\leq\alpha}=p^{-1}(Y_{\leq\alpha}).
Next define

B_{\alpha}^{h}\widetilde{X}:=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X|_{\leq\alpha},p|_{\leq\alpha}):=\beta_{Y_{\leq\alpha}}^{*}X|_{\leq\alpha},\quad\text{ where }\quad\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.17226pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern Recall that
\mathrm{res}(Y_{\leq\alpha})
is the base of a fiber bundle projection in the iterated fibration structure of
\mathrm{res}(Y),

\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\phi_{\alpha}\phantom{x}}B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)=\mathrm{res}(Y_{\leq\alpha}),

and let us define
\partial_{\alpha}^{h}\widetilde{X}:=\overline{p}^{-1}(\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)),
where
\overline{p}
is the map in (
5.2
).
We claim that there is a fiber bundle map
\phi_{\alpha}^{h}:\partial_{\alpha}^{h}\widetilde{X}\longrightarrow B_{\alpha}^{h}\widetilde{X}
that participates in a pull-back diagram

(5.4)

\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}^{h}\widetilde{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}^{h}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{p}}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}^{h}\widetilde{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{p}_{\leq\alpha}}
\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y).}

To prove the claim,
we will use that
\mathrm{res}(Y)
is obtained from
Y
by performing a radial blow-up of the singular strata of
Y
in any non-decreasing order.
If we denote by
\widetilde{Y}_{1}
the space obtained from
Y
by blowing-up all of the strata that come before
Y_{\alpha},
then the closure of the
pre-image of
Y_{\alpha}
in
\widetilde{Y}_{1}
is precisely
\mathrm{res}(Y_{\leq\alpha})=B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)

since we have blown-up all of the singular strata of
Y_{\leq\alpha}.
Let us denote the partial blow-down map
\widetilde{Y}_{1}\longrightarrow Y
by
\gamma_{1},
so we have

\textstyle{B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{Y_{\leq\alpha}}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{Y}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{1}}
\textstyle{Y_{\leq\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y.}

Next let
\widetilde{Y}_{2}
be the space obtained from
\widetilde{Y}_{1}
by blowing-up
B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y),

let
\gamma_{2}:\widetilde{Y}_{2}\longrightarrow\widetilde{Y}_{1}
denote the blow-down map, and let

\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{Y}_{2}=\gamma_{2}^{-1}(B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y))
be the boundary hypersurface resulting from the blow-up.
The restriction of
\gamma_{2}
to
\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{Y}_{2}
is a fiber bundle projection onto
B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)),
which we denote by
\gamma_{2,\alpha}.

Finally, as
\mathrm{res}(Y)
is obtained by performing blow-ups on
\widetilde{Y}_{2},
there is a blow-down map
\gamma_{3}:\mathrm{res}(Y)\longrightarrow\widetilde{Y}_{2}

whose restriction to
\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)
is a blow-down map
\gamma_{3,\alpha}:\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)\longrightarrow\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{Y}_{2}.

Thus we have

\textstyle{\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{3}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{Y}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{2}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{Y}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{1}}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{3,\alpha}}
\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{Y}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{2,\alpha}}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{Y_{\leq\alpha}}}
\textstyle{Y_{\leq\alpha}\phantom{\widetilde{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Let us denote the composition of the maps along the bottom of this diagram by
\psi_{\alpha}:\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)\longrightarrow Y_{\leq\alpha}.

Now notice that both of the fiber bundle maps

\bar{p}:\beta_{Y}^{*}X\longrightarrow\mathrm{res}(Y)\quad\text{ and }\quad\overline{p}_{\leq\alpha}:\beta_{Y_{\leq\alpha}}^{*}X|_{\leq\alpha}\longrightarrow\mathrm{res}(Y_{\leq\alpha})=B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)

are obtained by pulling-back
p:X\longrightarrow Y
in the former case and
p
restricted to
Y_{\leq\alpha}
in the latter case.
Thus they are related by

\textstyle{\beta_{Y}^{*}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{p}}
\textstyle{\psi_{\alpha}^{*}X|_{\leq\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\gamma_{1}^{*}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\beta_{Y_{\leq\alpha}}^{*}X|_{\leq\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{p}_{\leq\alpha}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{Y}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X|_{\leq\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)\;\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{Y_{\leq\alpha}}}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{Y_{\leq\alpha}\;\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

from which we recognize that the restriction of
\bar{p}:\beta_{Y}^{*}X\longrightarrow\mathrm{res}(Y)
to the pre-image of
\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)

is equal to the pull-back of
\overline{p}_{\leq\alpha}:\beta_{Y_{\leq\alpha}}^{*}X|_{\leq\alpha}\longrightarrow B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)
along
\phi_{\alpha}.
That is

\textstyle{\beta_{Y}^{*}X|_{\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{p}}
\textstyle{\beta_{Y_{\leq\alpha}}^{*}X|_{\leq\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{p}_{\leq\alpha}}
\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}\mathrm{res}(Y)}

is, as indicated, a pull-back diagram. Recognizing that this diagram is the same as (
5.4
) establishes the claim and produces the desired horizontal iterated fibration structure.
∎

It will happen that we will start out with a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces

W\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y

then we will resolve it to some extent in order to carry out analytic constructions but we will then want to make conclusions that refer back
to the original stratified spaces. For this reason it is convenient to be able to recognize the continuous functions on the stratified spaces among
the continuous functions on the resolutions.

Analogously to how we defined
\cal C_{\Phi}(\mathrm{res}(X))
in (
2.10
), we can define

(5.5)

\cal C_{\Phi-v}(\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X))=\{f\in\cal C(\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X)):\\
\text{ for all }a\in S(W),\text{ we have }f|_{\partial_{a}^{v}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X)}\in(\phi_{a}^{v})^{*}\cal C(B_{a}^{v}X)\},

\cal C_{\Phi-h}(\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X))=\{f\in\cal C(\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X)):\\
\text{ for all }\alpha\in S(Y),\text{ we have }f|_{\partial_{\alpha}^{h}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X)}\in(\phi_{\alpha}^{h})^{*}\cal C(B_{\alpha}^{h}X)\Big\},

and the analogous
\cal C_{\Phi-h,\Phi-v}(\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)),
and these too will be
*
-isomorphic to
\cal C(X).

Finally we point out that the Kottke-Rochon result mentioned above,
[
KR23
, Theorem 5.1]
, extends to fiber bundles. That is to say

\mathrm{res}(X)=[\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X);\text{ codim $2$ corners }]

where the codimension two corners are blown-up in any order consistent with the partial order on
S(X)=S(W)\times S(Y).
Indeed the proof of this result
in
loc cit
is coordinate based so it continues to hold in this setting.

Similarly, by choosing a connection for
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\widetilde{p}\phantom{x}}\mathrm{res}(Y)
we can consider a Riemannian metric on the interior of

\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)
of the form

g_{\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)/\mathrm{res}(Y)}\oplus\widetilde{p}^{*}g_{\mathrm{res}(Y)}

where the first summand is a family of wedge metrics on the fibers of
\widetilde{p}
and the second summand is the lift of a wedge metric on
\mathrm{res}(Y).

The argument in
[
KR23
, Alternate proof of Theorem 6.8]
shows that this metric lifts to
\mathrm{res}(X)
to be a non-degenerate and non-singular
bundle metric on

\beta_{\mathrm{res}(X)}^{*}\Big({}^{w}T\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)/\mathrm{res}(Y)\oplus{}^{w}T\mathrm{res}(Y)\Big),

where
\beta_{\mathrm{res}(X)}:\mathrm{res}(X)\longrightarrow\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)
is the blow-down map.
As pointed out in
loc cit
, it follows that this pull-back bundle is isomorphic to the wedge tangent bundle of
\mathrm{res}(X),

(5.6)

{}^{w}T\mathrm{res}(X)=\beta_{\mathrm{res}(X)}^{*}\Big({}^{w}T\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)/\mathrm{res}(Y)\oplus{}^{w}T\mathrm{res}(Y)\Big).

(Note that
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)
generally does not have an iterated fibration structure and hence does not have a wedge tangent bundle.)

In fact, Kottke-Rochon show that if the vertical and horizontal wedge metrics are product-type (which they call rigid), then so is the lift to
\mathrm{res}(X).

It follows that the same holds for totally-geodesic wedge metrics.

5.2.
Vertical wedge Dirac-type operators and the analytic transfer map

In this section we gather together the final pieces needed to assign a KK-class to a fiber bundle of oriented Witt pseudomanifolds.
More generally, we explain how a family of wedge Dirac-type operators defines a KK-class. Starting with a fiber bundle of
stratified spaces, we pass to the grid resolution to apply results from
[
AGR23
]
but we want the KK-class
to correspond to the initial stratified spaces. This is where the structure developed above comes in as it allows us to show
that the
C^{*}
-algebras of functions that we use on the resolved spaces can be identified with the continuous functions on
the stratified spaces.

Our first step is to describe the structure of wedge differential forms on a fiber bundle of manifolds with corners
L\;\operatorname{---}\;M\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\psi\phantom{x}}N
endowed
with a grid iteration fibration structure.
We shall have need of the
vertical wedge cotangent bundle
, defined as follows.
First recall that the vertical tangent bundle, which we denote
TM/N\longrightarrow M,
is the vector bundle given by the null space of
D\psi
and its restriction to any fiber

L_{q}=\psi^{-1}(q)
is equal to its tangent bundle
TL_{q}.
The vertical cotangent bundle, which we will denote
T^{*}M/N\longrightarrow M,

is the dual bundle to the vertical tangent bundle.
Finally, to define the vertical wedge cotangent bundle we proceed as above to first describe the vertical wedge one-forms,

\cal V_{w}^{*}(M/N)=\{\theta\in\cal C^{\infty}(M;T^{*}M/N):\text{ for each $a\in S(L)$ and $y\in B_{a}^{v}M,$}\quad\theta|_{\phi_{a}^{-1}(y)}=0\},

and then appeal to the Serre-Swan theorem to obtain the associated bundle, denoted

{}^{w}T^{*}M/N\longrightarrow M.

Next we consider how this construction relates to the horizontal iterated fibration structure.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
L\;\operatorname{---}\;M\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\psi\phantom{x}}N
be a fiber bundle of manifolds with corners endowed with a grid iteration fibration structure.

For every
\alpha\in S(N)
and every
k\geq 0,
we have a pull-back diagram

\textstyle{\Lambda^{k}({}^{w}T^{*}M/N)|_{\partial_{\alpha}N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Lambda^{k}({}^{w}T^{*}B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}^{h}}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}^{h}M}

where
\phi_{\alpha}^{h}:\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M\longrightarrow B_{\alpha}^{h}M
is the fiber bundle from Definition
5.1
(1).

Proof.

We first note that commutativity of the diagram (
5.1
) implies that the differential of
\phi_{\alpha}^{h},

D\phi_{\alpha}^{h}:T\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M\longrightarrow TB_{\alpha}^{h}M

restricts to a map between the vertical tangent bundles,

T(\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M/\partial_{\alpha}N)\longrightarrow T(B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N).

As (
5.1
) is a pull-back diagram,
D\phi_{\alpha}^{h}
sends each fiber of the vertical bundle of
\psi

isomorphically onto the corresponding fiber of the vertical bundle of
\psi_{\alpha}^{h}.
Indeed, if

\cal U\subseteq B_{\alpha}N
is an open set such that we may trivialize
\psi_{\alpha}^{h}
over
\cal U
and

\overline{p}
over
\phi_{\alpha}^{-1}(\cal U)
then the diagram restricts to

(5.7)

\textstyle{\phi_{\alpha}^{-1}(\cal U)\times L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}\times\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\cal U\times L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\phi_{\alpha}^{-1}(\cal U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\cal U}

where
L
is the typical fiber of
\psi_{\alpha}^{h}
(and
\psi
), and then the claimed behavior of
D\phi_{\alpha}^{h}
is clear.
It follows that the vertical tangent bundles participate in a pull-back diagram

(5.8)

\textstyle{T(\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M/\partial_{\alpha}N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T(B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}^{h}}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}^{h}M,}

i.e.,
(\phi_{\alpha}^{h})^{*}T(B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N)=T(\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M/\partial_{\alpha}N).

We can then obtain a bundle morphism

(\phi_{\alpha}^{h})^{*}T^{*}(B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N)\longrightarrow((\phi_{\alpha}^{h})^{*}T(B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N))^{*}=T^{*}(\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M/\partial_{\alpha}N)

by noting that any element

\theta\in(\phi_{\alpha}^{h})^{*}T^{*}(B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N))|_{\zeta\in\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M}

defines a functional on
T(\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M/\partial_{\alpha}N)|_{\zeta\in\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M}

since we have identified it with
T(B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N)|_{\phi_{\alpha}^{h}(\zeta)\in B_{\alpha}^{h}M}.

This bundle morphism restricts to an isomorphism between fibers and hence justifies the pull-back diagram

\textstyle{T^{*}(\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M/\partial_{\alpha}N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T^{*}(B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}^{h}}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}^{h}M.}

Again appealing to (
5.7
), we see that this identification restricts to a bijection between the vertical wedge one-forms on
\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M

and the vertical wedge one-forms on
B_{\alpha}^{h}M
and so induces a bundle isomorphism

(\phi_{\alpha}^{h})^{*}({}^{w}T^{*}(B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N))\cong{}^{w}T^{*}(\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M/\partial_{\alpha}N).

Finally, since pull-back commutes with taking exterior products, the statement of the proposition follows.
∎

A useful consequence of this proposition is that we can define vertical wedge bundles of the partial resolution corresponding to the fibers of a fiber
bundle of stratified spaces.

Remark 5.4
.

Let us consider what this says in the setting of the prelude from section
5.1
. We start with
W\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y,
a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces where the base and fiber have stratifications of depth one and we denote its grid resolution by

\widetilde{W}=\mathrm{res}(W)\;\operatorname{---}\;\widetilde{X}=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\widetilde{p}\phantom{x}}\widetilde{Y}=\mathrm{res}(Y).

As this is a fiber bundle of smooth manifolds, its vertical tangent bundle is
T\widetilde{X}\diagup\widetilde{W}=\mathrm{Ker}(D\widetilde{p})\subseteq T\widetilde{X}.
One consequence of (the proof of) Proposition
5.3
that we want to point out is that there is also vertical tangent bundle even if we only resolve the fibers. Indeed, there is a natural map
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{grid}}(X)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\beta^{h}_{X}\phantom{x}}\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X)
which collapses the fibers of
\partial_{0}^{h}\widetilde{X},
i.e., replaces
\partial_{0}^{h}\widetilde{X}
with
B_{0}^{h}\widetilde{X},
and (
5.8
) shows that the vertical tangent bundle is the pull-back of a vector bundle on
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X)
along
\beta^{h}_{X}.
The proposition points out that this is true also for the wedge cotangent bundle.

Definition 5.5

(Vertical cotangent bundle of vertical iterated fibration structures)
.

Let
L\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
be a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces, in which the fiber is a smooth manifold with corners, endowed with a vertical
iterated fibration structure, let

L\;\operatorname{---}\;M=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\overline{p}\phantom{x}}N=\mathrm{res}(Y)

be the partial resolution corresponding to the base, and let
\beta_{X}:M\longrightarrow X
denote the blow-down map. Since
M\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\overline{p}\phantom{x}}N
has a grid
iterated fibration structure, it follows from Proposition
5.3
that there is a vector bundle over
X,
which we will call the
vertical wedge cotangent bundle of
X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
and denote
{}^{w}T^{*}X/Y\longrightarrow X,
which pulls-back along
\beta_{X}
to the vertical wedge cotangent
bundle of
M\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\overline{p}\phantom{x}}N,

\textstyle{{}^{w}T^{*}M/N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{}^{w}T^{*}X/Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{X}}
\textstyle{X.}

The bundle
{}^{w}T^{*}X/Y\longrightarrow X
is smooth as a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces and restricts to each fiber of
p
to the cotangent bundle
of
L,

{}^{w}T^{*}X/Y|_{p^{-1}(y)}={}^{w}T^{*}L_{y}.

Of course one can similarly define the vertical wedge tangent bundle and the bundles of vertical wedge differential forms of a vertical iterated
fibration structure.

Our next objective is to obtain KK-classes from vertical families of Dirac-type operators associated to vertical iterated fibration structures, which
we will accomplish by applying results from
[
AGR23
]
. As this paper worked in the setting of fiber bundles of smooth manifolds with
corners we will also make use of grid iterated fibration structures.

Definition 5.6
.

(1)

Let
L\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
be a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces, in which the fiber is a smooth manifold with corners, endowed with a vertical
iterated fibration structure, let
g_{X/Y}
be a smooth family of (totally geodesic) wedge metrics on the fibers of
p,
i.e., a bundle metric
on the vertical wedge tangent bundle,
{}^{w}TX/Y,
which restricts to each fiber to be a totally geodesic wedge metric.
A
p
-vertical wedge Clifford structure
on
X
consists of
a smooth complex vector bundle
E\longrightarrow X
endowed with a Hermitian metric
g_{E},
a metric connection
\nabla^{E},

and a bundle homomorphism (referred to as Clifford multiplication)

\mathpzc{cl}:\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathrm{Cl}({}^{w}T^{*}X/Y,g_{X/Y})\longrightarrow\mathrm{End}(E)

compatible with the metric and the connection.

(2)

Given a fiber bundle of smooth manifolds with corners,
L\;\operatorname{---}\;M\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\psi\phantom{x}}N,
endowed with a grid iterated fibration structure,
a
horizontal system of vertical wedge Clifford structures
consists of a
\psi
-vertical wedge Clifford structure,

(g_{M/N},E_{M}\longrightarrow M,g_{E_{M}},\nabla^{E_{M}},\mathpzc{cl}),
and, for each
\alpha\in S(N)
with associated pull-back diagram of fiber bundles

\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}^{h}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}^{h}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\alpha}^{h}}
\textstyle{\partial_{\alpha}N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{B_{\alpha}N,}

a
\psi_{\alpha}^{h}
-vertical wedge Clifford structure
(g_{B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N},E_{\alpha}\longrightarrow B_{\alpha}^{h}M,g_{E_{\alpha}},\nabla^{E_{\alpha}},\mathpzc{cl}),
such that these satisfy

(\phi_{\alpha}^{h})^{*}(g_{B_{\alpha}^{h}M/B_{\alpha}N},E_{\alpha}\longrightarrow B_{\alpha}^{h}M,g_{E_{\alpha}},\nabla^{E_{\alpha}},\mathpzc{cl})=(g_{M/N},E_{M}\longrightarrow M,g_{E_{M}},\nabla^{E_{M}},\mathpzc{cl})|_{B_{\alpha}^{h}M}.

Note that Proposition
5.3
is implicitly used in asking for the Clifford multiplication on
\partial_{\alpha}^{h}M

to be the pull-back of the Clifford multiplication on
B_{\alpha}^{h}M.
The same proposition also shows that the bundle of wedge
differential forms makes up part of a horizontal system of vertical wedge Clifford structures, which we can complete by
picking a suitable vertical wedge metric. The fact that this horizontal system is pulled-back from a vertical iterated fibration structure is no coincidence.
Indeed, immediately from the definition we see that if
L\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
has a vertical iterated fibration structure,

L\;\operatorname{---}\;M=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{base}}(X,p)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\overline{p}\phantom{x}}N=\mathrm{res}(Y)

is the partial resolution corresponding to the base, and
\beta_{X}:M\longrightarrow X
is the blow-down map, then any
p
-vertical wedge Clifford structure

(g_{X/Y},E\longrightarrow X,g_{E},\nabla^{E},\mathpzc{cl})
can be pulled-back along
\beta_{X}
to produce a horizontal system of vertical wedge Clifford structures.
It is also easy to see that all horizontal systems can be obtained in this way.

Associated to a vertical iterated fibration structure,
L\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y,
endowed with a
p
-vertical wedge Clifford structure is a family of wedge Dirac-type operators,

Y\ni y\mapsto\eth^{E}_{L_{y}}

defined as in §
3.2
, which we denote
\eth^{E}_{X/Y}.
We denote the realization of this family acting on
L^{2}
sections of
E
with the vertical APS domain by
\eth^{E}_{X/Y,VAPS}.

Similarly if
L\;\operatorname{---}\;M\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\psi\phantom{x}}N
is a fiber bundle of smooth manifolds with corners endowed with a grid iterated fibration structure and a
horizontal system of vertical wedge Clifford structures, we can define a family of wedge Dirac-type operators in the same way. We denote the resulting
family by
\eth^{E}_{M/N}
and the realization of this family acting on
L^{2}
sections of
E
with the vertical APS domain by
\eth^{E}_{M/N,VAPS}.

In either case we say that the family of Dirac-type operators satisfies the analytic Witt condition
if the individual Dirac-type operators satisfy the condition
(as described in §
3.2
).

Theorem 5.7
.

[
AGR23
, Theorem 1]

Let
L\;\operatorname{---}\;M\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\psi\phantom{x}}N
be a fiber bundle of smooth manifolds with corners endowed with a
\psi
-vertical iterated fibration structure and a
\psi
-vertical wedge Clifford structure. If the corresponding family of Dirac-type operators
\eth^{E}_{M/N,VAPS}
satisfies the analytic Witt condition then it is a family of self-adjoint Fredholm operators with compact resolvent.

This theorem does not require a grid iterated fibration structure but it does require that the spaces involved
are all smooth manifolds with corners. However by applying it to
fiber bundles
L\;\operatorname{---}\;M\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\psi\phantom{x}}N
of smooth manifolds with corners that are endowed with a grid iterated fibration structures and a
horizontal system of vertical wedge Clifford structures, we can obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 5.8
.

Let
L\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
be a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces, in which the fibers are smooth manifolds with corners, endowed with a
p
-vertical wedge Clifford structure.
If the corresponding family of Dirac-type operators
\eth^{E}_{X/Y,VAPS}
satisfies the analytic Witt condition then it is a family of self-adjoint Fredholm operators with
compact resolvent.

This corollary is the result we have been aiming for. Our aim has been to assign a KK-class to a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified Witt spaces by means of the vertical signature operators (to be realized in Corollary
5.10
below). This leads us to using the grid resolution for a couple of reasons: first, defining Clifford structures requires tangent bundles and, second, the results (and proofs) in
[
AGR23
]
assume fiber bundles of smooth manifolds with corners. What we have shown is that we can start with a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces, resolve only the fibers, and still make sense of and apply the results of
[
AGR23
]
. We will now carry out the usual repackaging of these results to obtain an unbounded cycle and define a KK-class. (The final result will be nicest if we start with a fiber bundle in which all of the space involved are potentially singular spaces and we assume that the Clifford structure is defined on the partial resolution of the fibers.)

Thus let
Z\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
be a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces and let

L:=\mathrm{res}(Z)\;\operatorname{---}\;\widehat{X}:=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\hat{p}\phantom{x}}Y

be the partial resolution with respect to the fibers.
Suppose that
L\;\operatorname{---}\;\widehat{X}\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\hat{p}\phantom{x}}Y
is endowed with a
p
-vertical wedge Clifford structure

(g_{\widehat{X}/Y},E\longrightarrow\widehat{X},g_{E},\nabla^{E},\mathpzc{cl})
whose associated Dirac-type operators satisfy the analytic Witt condition.
Let us endow

\cal E^{\prime}=\cal C_{c}(\hat{X}_{\mathrm{reg}};E)

with the
\cal C_{0}(Y)
-valued inner product defined by

\textstyle{\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\cal E^{\prime}}:}
\textstyle{\cal E^{\prime}\times\cal E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal C_{0}(Y)}
\textstyle{(f,h)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Big(y\mapsto\int_{p^{-1}(y)}g_{E}(f(\zeta),h(\zeta))\;\mathrm{dvol}_{g_{L_{y}}}\Big)}

and then define
\cal E
to be the closure of
\cal E^{\prime}
with respect to the resulting norm.
Since
\cal E
is a Hilbert module over
\cal C_{0}(Y)
it can be identified with the space of continuous sections vanishing at infinity of a continuous field
of Hilbert spaces over
Y.
In this case, of the Hilbert bundle

L^{2}(L;E)-L^{2}(\widehat{X}/Y;E)\longrightarrow Y

induced by
p,
so that we have

\cal E=\cal C_{0}(Y;L^{2}(\widehat{X}/Y;E)).

We let
\cal C_{\Phi-v}(\widehat{X})
(defined in (
5.5
)) act on
\cal E
by left multiplication and
\cal C(Y)
act on
\cal E
by right
multiplication and we obtain a
\cal C_{\Phi-v}(\widehat{X})
-
\cal C(Y)
Hilbert bi-module,
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded if
E
is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded.
Define
\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y}
to be the operator on
\cal E
given by

(5.9)

(\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y}\omega)(y)=\eth_{L_{y},VAPS}(\omega(y)),\\
\text{ for all }\omega\in\mathrm{dom}(\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y})=\{\omega\in\cal E:\omega(y)\in\cal D_{VAPS}(\eth^{E}_{L_{y}})\;\&\;\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y}\omega\in\cal E\}.

Since
\eth^{E}_{\widehat{X}/Y,VAPS}
is closed, densely defined and
\mathrm{dom}(\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y})|_{y\in Y}
is dense in

\mathrm{dom}(\eth^{E}_{L_{y}})
(in fact they are equal), it follows from
[
Hil89
, Proposition 2.9]
that
\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y}

is self-adjoint and regular.

Next note that elements
h\in\cal C^{1}_{\Phi-v}(\widehat{X})
are such that
[\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y},h]
is adjointable and bounded
and such that multiplication by
h
preserves the VAPS-domain. Hence

\cal C^{1}_{\Phi-v}(\widehat{X})\subseteq\mathrm{Lip}(\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y})
and

\mathrm{Lip}(\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y})=\mathrm{Lip}_{\cal K}(^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y})
since the resolvent of each
\eth^{E}_{L_{y},VAPS}
is
compact. Thus the closure of
\mathrm{Lip}_{\cal K}(^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y})
contains the multiplication operators acting by elements of

\cal C_{\Phi-v}(\widehat{X}).
This shows that these data define an unbounded
\cal C_{\Phi-v}(\widehat{X})
-
\cal C(Y)
-cycle,

[\cal C_{0}(Y;L^{2}(\widehat{X}/Y;E)),\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y}]\in\overline{\mathrm{UKK}}^{\dim Z}(\cal C_{\Phi-v}(\widehat{X}),\cal C(Y)).

If
X
is not compact, but the links of
Z
are compact, then as discussed at the end of §
3.2
we can define a KK-class using
functions that vanish at infinity.
Finally, since
\cal C_{\Phi-v}(\widehat{X})
is
*
-isomorphic to
\cal C(X)
and the unbounded
\mathrm{KK}
-groups coincide with those defined
by Kasparov we can summarize this as folllows.

Corollary 5.9
.

If
Z\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
is a fiber bundle of smoothly stratified spaces,
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\hat{p}\phantom{x}}Y
is endowed with a
\hat{p}
-vertical wedge Clifford structure whose associated Dirac-type operators satisfy the analytic Witt condition and
\cal E,

\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y}
are defined as above then these data define

[\eth^{\cal E}_{\widehat{X}/Y}]\in\mathrm{KK}^{\dim X/Y}(\cal C_{0}(X),\cal C_{0}(Y)).

The case of most interest to us comes from specializing this corollary to the signature operator.

Corollary 5.10
.

To any smooth oriented fiber bundle
p:X\longrightarrow Y
of stratified spaces, whose typical fiber
Z
is a Witt pseudomanifold, there is associated a KK-class

[D^{\mathrm{sign}}_{X/Y}]\in\mathrm{KK}^{\dim X/Y}(\cal C_{0}(X),\cal C_{0}(Y))

given by the KK-class of the family of signature operators associated to any totally geodesic vertical wedge metric.

Proof.

For any choice of totally geodesic vertical wedge metric Corollary
5.9
defines a KK-class from the family of signature operators
of the resolutions of the fibers of
p,
so we only need to justify that this class is independent of the choice of metric, and so it suffices to note that
any two appropriate totally geodesic vertical wedge metric can be connected by a homotopy which induces a homotopy of the corresponding families of
signature operators and hence does not affect the KK-class.
∎

Just as we defined the analytic orientation by normalizing the K-homology class of the signature operator after inverting
2,
so too do we define the analytic transfer class in KK-theory as a normalized version of the class of the vertical family of signature operators after inverting
2.
This has two advantages: first, product formulas will hold ‘on the nose’ without occasional factors of two (see Corollary
5.17
) and secondly, to be consistent with the K-homology class of the signature operator when
Y
is a point (see Proposition
5.13
).

Definition 5.11
.

Let
p:X\longrightarrow Y
be a smooth oriented fiber bundle of stratified spaces whose typical fiber
Z
is a Witt pseudomanifold. We define
the analytic transfer class associated to
p
to be
13
13

13

NB: In the setting of Lipschitz manifolds, Hilsum
[
Hil89
, §2.4]
uses the same notation with a slightly different meaning: for him
\Sigma(p)
denotes the class of
[D^{\mathrm{sign}}_{X/Y}].

\Sigma(p)=2^{-\lfloor\dim Z/2\rfloor}[D^{\mathrm{sign}}_{X/Y}]\in\mathrm{KK}^{\dim X/Y}(\cal C_{0}(X),\cal C_{0}(Y))[\tfrac{1}{2}].

Another important special case is when the partial resolution corresponding to the fibers of the fiber bundle
Z\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y,

\hat{X}=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\hat{p}\phantom{x}}Y

admits a vertical wedge spin structure; that is, when the vertical wedge tangent bundle
{}^{w}T\hat{X}/Y
is spin.
Choosing a spin bundle and a vertical wedge metric we obtain a family of spin Dirac operators and if they satisfy the analytic Witt condition then we obtain a KK-class.

Corollary 5.12
.

To any smooth fiber bundle
p:X\longrightarrow Y
of smoothly stratified spaces, whose vertical wedge tangent bundle
{}^{w}T\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X,p)/Y
is endowed with a spin structure, there is for each vertical wedge metric
g
an associated a KK-class

[D^{\mathrm{spin}}_{X/Y}]\in\mathrm{KK}^{\mathrm{dimX/Y}}(\cal C_{0}(X),\cal C_{0}(Y))

given by the KK-class of the corresponding family of spin Dirac operators.

Unlike for smooth manifolds, the class of the spin Dirac operator in KK-theory will depend on the choice of wedge metric. Indeed, whether or not the spin Dirac operator is Fredholm will depend on the choice of metric.

A natural condition that ensures that the analytic Witt condition holds is known as ‘psc-Witt’. Suppose that
(X,g)
is a smoothly stratified pseudomanifold all of whose strata are spin, endowed with wedge metric
g,
we say that the
psc-Witt condition
holds if the induced metrics on the links of
X
have positive scalar curvature (see
[
BPR21
,
BPR23
]
). Since all of the strata are spin, the boundary family at each stratum consists of the spin Dirac operators of the links and hence the Lichnerowicz formula shows that the psc-Witt condition implies the analytic Witt condition.
A stronger sufficient condition is to assume that the wedge metric has non-negative scalar curvature near the singularities; this implies that the operators are also essentially self-adjoint (see, e.g.,
[
AGR16
, §7]
).
(One can analogously treat spin-c structures; the analogue of the psc-Witt condition is the ‘generalized psc-Witt condition’ see
[
BR24
]
.)

Note that if in the fiber bundle
Z\;\operatorname{---}\;X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
the base
Y
consists of a single point then the construction of the unbounded

\cal C_{\Phi-v}(\widehat{X})
-
\cal C(Y)
-cycle coincides with the construction of the KK-class associated to a single Dirac-type operator in
§
3.3
. The special case of the signature operator is important enough for us that we label it as a proposition.

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
X^{n}
be an orientable compact closed
n
-dimensional smoothly stratified Witt pseudomanifold. Then the Gysin element of the constant map

h:X\to\operatorname{pt}
agrees with the class of the signature operator,

\Sigma(h)=\mathrm{sign}_{K}(X)\;\;\text{in}\;\;\mathrm{K}_{n}(X)[\tfrac{1}{2}]

Definition 5.14
.

The
analytic transfer maps
associated to any smooth oriented fiber bundle
p:X\longrightarrow Y
of stratified spaces, whose typical fiber
Z
is an oriented Witt pseudomanifold, are the maps induced by Kasparov product with
\Sigma(p).

We denote the corresponding maps in K-homology by

\textstyle{\mathrm{K}_{*}^{{\operatorname{an}}}(Y)[\tfrac{1}{2}]=\mathrm{KK}^{*}(C_{0}(Y),\mathbb{C})[\tfrac{1}{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{!}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{*}(C_{0}(X),\mathbb{C})[\tfrac{1}{2}]=\mathrm{K}_{*}^{{\operatorname{an}}}(X)[\tfrac{1}{2}]}
\textstyle{[A]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma(p)\otimes[A],}

and the corresponding map in topological K-theory by

\textstyle{\mathrm{K}^{*}(Y)[\tfrac{1}{2}]=\mathrm{KK}^{*}(\mathbb{C},C_{0}(X))[\tfrac{1}{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{!}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{*}(\mathbb{C},C_{0}(Y))[\tfrac{1}{2}]=\mathrm{K}^{*}(Y)[\tfrac{1}{2}]}
\textstyle{[A]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[A]\otimes\Sigma(p)}

where
\otimes
denotes the Kasparov product.

5.3.
Functoriality for fibrations

In this section our aim is to start with a commutative diagram of fiber bundles of smoothly stratified spaces

(5.10)

\textstyle{X_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{12}}
\scriptstyle{p_{13}}
\textstyle{X_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{23}}
\textstyle{X_{3}}

and enough data to define appropriate Dirac-type operators and then to understand the relation between the corresponding

\mathrm{KK}
-classes.
In particular we will generalize
[
RW06
, Lemma 6]
and relate the analytic signature class of
p_{13}
with the Kasparov product of those of
p_{12}
and
p_{23}
in Corollary
5.17
.
An important special case corresponds to taking
X_{3}
to be a single point:

Theorem 5.15
.

If
X\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}p\phantom{x}}Y
is an oriented fiber bundle
map of Witt pseudomanifolds, then

\mathrm{sign}_{K}(X)=\Sigma(p)\otimes\mathrm{sign}_{K}(Y)=p^{!}\mathrm{sign}_{K}(Y)\quad\text{ in }\mathrm{K}_{\dim X}^{\mathrm{an}}(X)[\tfrac{1}{2}]

i.e., the analytic transfer map takes the signature orientation of the base
Y
to the signature orientation of the total space
X.

A special case of this latter result, when
X\longrightarrow Y
is a real oriented vector bundle over a Witt pseudomanifold, was worked out by Hilsum in

[
Hil14
, Théorème 4.4, pg. 187]
.

Our proof of the functoriality, just as proofs of similar statements for smooth manifolds in, e.g.,
[
KvS18
,
vdD22
]
,
consists of relating the corresponding Dirac-type operators and then using Kucerovsky’s unbounded version of the Connes-Skandalis
characterization of the Kasparov product
[
Kuc97
]
.

We will show something more general than the relation between the analytic signature classes, namely that data sufficient to define a family of Dirac-type operators for
p_{12}
and
p_{23}
are also sufficient to define a family of Dirac-type operators for
p_{13}
and that the resulting KK-classes are related by Kasparov product. To state this more precisely let us start by denoting the typical fiber of the map
p_{ij}
by
F_{ij}
so that

\begin{gathered}\mathrm{res}(F_{12})\;\operatorname{---}\;\widehat{X}_{1}:=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{12})\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\widehat{p}_{12}\phantom{x}}X_{2},\\
\mathrm{res}(F_{23})\;\operatorname{---}\;\widehat{X}_{2}:=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{2},p_{23})\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\widehat{p}_{23}\phantom{x}}X_{3},\\
\mathrm{res}(F_{13})=\mathrm{res}(F_{12})\widetilde{\times}\mathrm{res}(F_{23})\;\operatorname{---}\;\widetilde{X}_{1}:=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{13})\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\widehat{p}_{13}\phantom{x}}X_{3},\end{gathered}

where
\widetilde{\times}
denotes the ordered product of Kottke-Rochon
[
KR23
]
mentioned in §
5.1
.
Locally over an open set
\cal U\subseteq X_{3}
on which we can trivialize all of the
p_{ij}
we have natural maps

\textstyle{\mathrm{res}(F_{12})\widetilde{\times}\mathrm{res}(F_{23})\times\cal U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{res}(F_{12})\times F_{23}\times\cal U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{res}(F_{23})\times\cal U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{23}\times\cal U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cal U}

and these maps fit together into the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{13})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{12})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{2},p_{23})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{3}.}

The bundle

(5.11)

\alpha^{*}{}^{w}T^{*}\widehat{X}_{1}/X_{2}\oplus\beta^{*}{}^{w}T^{*}\widehat{X}_{2}/X_{3}\longrightarrow\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{13})

is, by
[
KR23
, Alternate proof of Theorem 6.8]
mentioned above (
5.6
), the vertical wedge cotangent
bundle of
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{13})\longrightarrow X_{3},

{}^{w}T^{*}\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}.

Thus vertical wedge metrics for
\widehat{X}_{1}\longrightarrow X_{2}
and
\widehat{X}_{2}\longrightarrow X_{3}
induce a vertical wedge metric for
\widetilde{X}_{1}\longrightarrow X_{3}.

If we are given

•

a vertical wedge Clifford structure

(g_{\widehat{X}_{1}/X_{2}},E_{12}\longrightarrow\widehat{X}_{1},g_{E_{12}},\nabla^{E_{12}},\mathpzc{cl}),

for
\widehat{X}_{1}=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{12})\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\widehat{p}_{12}\phantom{x}}X_{2},

•

a vertical wedge Clifford structure

(g_{\widehat{X}_{2}/X_{3}},E_{23}\longrightarrow\widehat{X}_{2},g_{E_{23}},\nabla^{E_{23}},\mathpzc{cl}),

for
\widehat{X}_{2}=\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{2},p_{23})\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\widehat{p}_{23}\phantom{x}}X_{3},

then we define a bundle
E_{13}\longrightarrow\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{13})
by

E_{13}=\alpha^{*}E_{12}\widehat{\otimes}\beta^{*}E_{23},

where
\widehat{\otimes}
denotes the graded tensor product
14
14

14

The graded tensor product of Clifford modules is discussed in, e.g.,

[
ABS64
, §6]
.
.
There is an induced bundle metric
g_{E_{13}}=\alpha^{*}g_{E_{12}}\otimes\beta^{*}g_{E_{23}},
an induced connection

\nabla^{E_{13},\oplus}=\alpha^{*}\nabla^{E_{12}}\otimes 1+1\otimes\beta^{*}\nabla^{E_{23}},

and an induced Clifford action: at each
\zeta\in\widetilde{X}_{1},

\begin{gathered}\text{ for any }\theta\otimes\eta\in\mathbb{C}l(\alpha^{*}{}^{w}T^{*}_{\alpha(\zeta)}\widehat{X}_{1}/X_{2})\widehat{\otimes}\mathbb{C}l(\beta^{*}{}^{w}T^{*}_{\beta(\zeta)}\widehat{X}_{2}/X_{3}),\\
\text{ and }\sigma\otimes\tau\in\alpha^{*}(E_{12})_{\alpha(\zeta)}\widehat{\otimes}\beta^{*}(E_{23})_{\beta(\zeta)},\\
\mathpzc{cl}(\theta\otimes\eta)(\sigma\otimes\tau)=(-1)^{\mathrm{deg}(\omega)\mathrm{deg}(\sigma)}\mathpzc{cl}(\theta)\sigma\otimes\mathpzc{cl}(\eta)\tau.\end{gathered}

Thanks to the identification of
{}^{w}T^{*}\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}
with
\alpha^{*}{}^{w}T^{*}\widehat{X}_{1}/X_{2}\oplus\beta^{*}{}^{w}T^{*}\widehat{X}_{2}/X_{3},
this
defines an action of the complexified Clifford algebra of
{}^{w}T^{*}\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}
on
E_{13}.

Thus we almost have an induced vertical Clifford structure for
\widetilde{X}_{1}\longrightarrow X_{3}.
The only issue is that
\nabla^{E_{13},\oplus}
is a Clifford connection
with respect to the connection

\nabla^{\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3},\oplus}=\alpha^{*}\nabla^{\hat{X}_{1}/X_{2}}\otimes 1+1\otimes\beta^{*}\nabla^{\hat{X}_{2}/X_{3}},

and this is generally not equal to the vertical Levi-Civita connection determined by
g_{\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}},

\nabla^{\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}}.

If we denote the difference between the two connections by

\nabla^{\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}}-\nabla^{\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3},\oplus}=\omega

then
\omega
is an one-form valued in the endomorphisms of vertical tangent bundle.
We can regard
\omega
as a
T^{*}\widetilde{X}_{1}
-valued vertical wedge two form using the identification

(\omega(V_{1},V_{2}),V_{3})=g_{\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}}((\nabla^{\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}}_{V_{3}}-\nabla^{\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3},\oplus}_{V_{3}})V_{1},V_{2}),

where
V_{3}\in\cal C^{\infty}(\widetilde{X}_{1};T\widetilde{X}_{1})
and
V_{1},V_{2}\in\cal C^{\infty}(\widetilde{X}_{1};{}^{w}T\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}),
and then define

\mathpzc{cl}(\omega)\in\cal C^{\infty}(\widetilde{X}_{1};T^{*}\widetilde{X}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{C}l({}^{w}T^{*}\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3})),\quad\mathpzc{cl}(\omega)=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{a,b}\omega(e_{a},e_{b})\otimes\mathpzc{cl}(e^{a})\mathpzc{cl}(e^{b}),

where
e_{a}
runs over an orthonormal basis of
{}^{w}T\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3},

and it follows from
[
BGV04
, Proposition 10.12]
that

\nabla^{E_{13}}:=\nabla^{E_{13},\oplus}+\mathpzc{cl}(\omega)

is a Clifford connection with respect to
\nabla^{\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}}.
We refer to the data defined in this way,

(g_{\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}},E_{13}\longrightarrow\widetilde{X}_{1},g_{E_{13}},\nabla^{E_{13}},\mathpzc{cl}),
as
the induced vertical Clifford structure for the fiber bundle
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{13})\longrightarrow X_{3}.

Finally, if the vertical wedge Clifford structures for
\hat{X}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\hat{p}_{12}\phantom{x}}X_{2}
and
\hat{X}_{2}\xrightarrow{\phantom{x}\hat{p}_{23}\phantom{x}}X_{3}
are
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded then we endow the vertical wedge Clifford structure for
\hat{X}_{1}\longrightarrow{\hat{p}_{13}}X_{3}
with the natural
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading. If neither of these is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded then we endow the vertical wedge Clifford structure for
\hat{X}_{1}\longrightarrow{\hat{p}_{13}}X_{3}
with the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading obtained by considering the ungraded Clifford structures as
\mathbb{C}l(1)
structures in the usual way (see, e.g.,
[
vSV22
, Appendix A]
and
[
BMvS16
, Examples 2.38-2.40]
).

Theorem 5.16
.

Suppose we are given fiber bundles of smoothly stratified spaces as in (
5.10
) and vertical Clifford structures for

\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{12})\longrightarrow X_{2}
and
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{2},p_{23})\longrightarrow X_{3},
and suppose that the families of Dirac-type operators of these two families, as well as that of the induced vertical Clifford structure for
\mathrm{res}_{\mathrm{fib}}(X_{1},p_{13})\longrightarrow X_{3},
satisfy the analytic Witt condition. Then the associated
\mathrm{KK}
-classes satisfy

[\eth^{\cal E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}]=[\eth^{\cal E_{12}}_{X_{1}/X_{2}}]\otimes[\eth^{\cal E_{23}}_{X_{2}/X_{3}}]\in\mathrm{KK}^{\dim X_{1}/X_{3}}(C_{0}(X_{1}),C_{0}(X_{3})).

Proof.

For simplicity, let us first assume that
\dim X_{1}/X_{2}
and
\dim X_{2}/X_{3}
are even.

Let
(\cal E_{ij},\eth^{\cal E_{ij}}_{X_{i}/X_{j}}),
with
(i,j)\in\{(1,2),(2,3),(1,3)\},
denote the three unbounded cycles. Recall that

\cal E_{ij}
is defined as the closure of
\cal C_{c}(X_{i,\mathrm{reg}};E_{ij})
with respect to the
\cal C_{0}(X_{j})
-valued inner product induced by
integation along the fibers
X_{i}/X_{j}.

Step 1
: We can assume without loss of generality that, for each
\zeta\in X_{2},

(5.12)

\cal D_{VAPS}(\eth_{F_{12,\zeta}}^{E})=\rho H^{1}_{e}(F_{12,\zeta};E).

Indeed, it is pointed out in
[
AGR16
, Remark 4.9]
that any family of wedge Dirac-type operators satisfying the analytic Witt condition is homotopic
through Fredholm families of wedge Dirac-type operators satisfying the analytic Witt condition to one whose ‘‘indicial roots” are as large as desired.
15
15

15

As
noted in
[
AGR16
, Remark 4.9]
the deformation is through Dirac-type operators but other structures are not necessarily preserved. For example
it is not necessarily possible to arrange (
5.12
) for the signature operator. Indeed a simple computation such as
[
ALMP12
, Lemma 5.6]
shows that (
5.12
) for the signature operator requires a “thick Witt condition”:

\mathrm{IH}_{\overline{m}}^{k}(Z)=0\text{ if }|\dim Z-2k|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}.

(This seems to have been overlooked in
[
HLV18
]
and this hypothesis should be included in their Theorem 1.1.)
However it is possible to deform the signature operator to a Dirac-type operator satisfying (
5.12
).

Since this does not change the KK-class of the family of Dirac-type operators we do not lose any generality by assuming that this condition holds.

Step 2
: We can identify
\cal E_{12}\otimes_{\cal C(X_{2})}\cal E_{23}
with
\cal E_{13}.

Recall (e.g., from
[
Lan95
, Chapter 4]
) that the interior tensor product used here is defined by first endowing the algebraic tensor product

\cal E_{12}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\cal E_{23}
with the inner product given on simple tensors by

\langle f\otimes h,f^{\prime}\otimes h^{\prime}\rangle=\langle h,\langle f,f^{\prime}\rangle h^{\prime}\rangle,

modding out by
N=\{z\in\cal E_{12}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\cal E_{23}:\langle z,z\rangle=0\}
and then completing with respect to this inner product.
In our setting this inner product is given on simple tensors with
f,f^{\prime}\in\cal E_{12}
and
h,h^{\prime}\in\cal E_{23}
by

X_{3}\ni q\mapsto\langle f\otimes h,f^{\prime}\otimes h^{\prime}\rangle(q)=\int_{r\in\widehat{p}_{23}^{-1}(q)}g_{E_{23}}(h(r),\langle f,f^{\prime}\rangle(r)h^{\prime}(r))\;\mathrm{dvol}_{\mathrm{res}(F_{23,q})}\\
=\int_{r\in\widehat{p}_{23}^{-1}(q)}g_{E_{23}}\Big(h(r),\big(\int_{s\in\widehat{p}_{12}^{-1}(r)}g_{E_{12}}(f(s),f^{\prime}(s))\;\mathrm{dvol}_{\mathrm{res}(F_{12,r})}\big)h^{\prime}(r)\Big)\;\mathrm{dvol}_{\mathrm{res}(F_{23,q})}.

Notice, directly from the definition of the
\cal E_{ij},
that we obtain the same completion if we start with the algebraic tensor product

\cal C_{c}(X_{1,\mathrm{reg}};E_{12})\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\cal C_{c}(X_{2,\mathrm{reg}};E_{23})
and that then we may rewrite the inner product
as an integral over the fibers of
\hat{p}_{13}:\widetilde{X}_{1}\longrightarrow X_{3},

X_{3}\ni q\mapsto\langle f\otimes h,f^{\prime}\otimes h^{\prime}\rangle(q)\\
=\int_{\hat{p}_{13}^{-1}(q)}g_{E_{12}}(\alpha^{*}f,\alpha^{*}f^{\prime})g_{E_{23}}(\beta^{*}h,\beta^{*}h^{\prime})\;\mathrm{dvol}_{F_{12,p_{23}^{-1}(q)}}\;\beta^{*}\mathrm{dvol}_{F_{23,q}}\\
=\int_{\hat{p}_{13}^{-1}(q)}g_{\alpha^{*}E_{12}\otimes\beta^{*}E_{23}}(f\otimes\beta^{*}h,f^{\prime}\otimes\beta^{*}h^{\prime})\;\mathrm{dvol}_{F_{13,q}},

where we have used that
\alpha|_{\widetilde{X}_{1,\mathrm{reg}}}=\mathrm{id}.
Thus the completion with respect to this inner product produces

\cal C(X_{3},L^{2}(X_{1,\mathrm{reg}}/X_{3}))=\cal C(X_{3},L^{2}(\widetilde{X}_{1}/X_{3}))=\cal E_{13},
as promised.

Step 3
: We obtain a local expression relating the Dirac-type operators.

This is straightforward since we may, by localizing on
X_{3}
and restricting to the regular parts, reduce to relating the the Dirac-type operator
on the total space of the fiber bundle of smooth manifolds,

F_{12,\mathrm{reg}}\;\operatorname{---}\;F_{13,\mathrm{reg}}\longrightarrow F_{23,\mathrm{reg}},

with the family of Dirac-type operators on the fibers and a lift of the Dirac-type operator on the base and this is carried out in Proposition 10.12 and
Theorem 10.19 in
[
BGV04
]
(see also
[
KvS18
, Theorem 22]
,
[
KvS20
, Theorem 30]
).
Thus we may write, as differential operators acting on smooth sections over the regular part of
X_{1},

\eth^{E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}=\eth^{E_{12}}_{X_{1}/X_{2}}+\widetilde{\eth}^{E_{23}}_{X_{2}/X_{3}}-\tfrac{1}{4}\sum_{a<b}\sum_{c}g_{X_{1}/X_{2}}([f_{a},f_{b}],e_{c})\mathpzc{cl}(f^{a})\mathpzc{cl}(f^{b})\mathpzc{cl}(e^{c})

where
\widetilde{\eth}^{E_{23}}_{X_{2}/X_{3}}
is a lift of
\eth^{E_{23}}_{X_{2}/X_{3}}
to
X_{1},
and in the final sum
(f_{a})
runs over an orthonormal frame
for
{}^{w}TX_{2}/X_{3},
lifted to
X_{1}
and
(e_{c})
runs over an orthonormal frame for
{}^{w}TX_{1}/X_{2}.

Let us define

D^{E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}=\eth^{E_{12}}_{X_{1}/X_{2}}+\widetilde{\eth}^{E_{23}}_{X_{2}/X_{3}}

and point out that
D^{E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}
is a relatively compact perturbation of
\eth^{E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}
when both are endowed with the VAPS
domain of the latter since from, e.g.,
[
AGR23
, §1.2]
, the final sum in the expression for
\eth^{E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}
is a uniformly bounded
section of the endomorphism bundle of
E_{13}
and, from
[
AGR23
, Theorem 1]
, the VAPS domain includes compactly into
L^{2}.

In particular, since the domain for
\eth^{\cal E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}
is pointwise equal to the VAPS domain of
\eth^{E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}
we can replace
this operator with
D^{E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}},
with the same domain, and obtain the same class in
\mathrm{KK}^{*}(C_{0}(X_{1}),C_{0}(X_{3})).

Step 4
: We use the characterization of the Kasparov product from
[
Kuc97
]
.

To prove the theorem it is sufficient, following
loc cit
, to show

i)

(connection) for all
\xi
in a dense subset of
\cal C(X_{1})\cdot\cal C(X_{2},L^{2}(X_{1}/X_{2};E_{12})),
the operator

\left[\begin{pmatrix}0&T_{\xi}^{*}\\
T_{\xi}&0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\eth^{\cal E_{23}}_{X_{2}/X_{3}}&0\\
0&D^{\cal E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}\end{pmatrix}\right],\text{ where }T_{\xi}(e)=\xi\otimes e

is bounded on
\mathrm{dom}(\eth^{\cal E_{23}}_{X_{2}/X_{3}}\oplus D^{\cal E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}),

ii)

(compatibility) there is a dense submodule
\cal W
of
\cal E_{13}
such that, for any
\mu_{13},\mu_{12}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\},

\eth^{\cal E_{12}}_{X_{1}/X_{2}}(i\mu_{13}+D^{\cal E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}})^{-1}(i\mu_{12}+\eth^{\cal E_{12}}_{X_{1}/X_{2}})^{-1}

is defined on
\cal W.

iii)

(positivity) for any
\mu_{13},\mu_{12}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\},
there exists
\lambda>0
such that

\langle(\eth^{\cal E_{12}}_{X_{1}/X_{2}}\otimes 1)\xi,D^{\cal E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}\xi\rangle+\langle D^{\cal E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}\xi,(\eth^{\cal E_{12}}_{X_{1}/X_{2}}\otimes 1)\xi\rangle\geq-\lambda\langle\xi,\xi\rangle,

for all
\xi\in(i\mu_{13}+D^{\cal E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}})^{-1}(i\mu_{12}+\eth^{\cal E_{12}}_{X_{1}/X_{2}})^{-1}\cal W.

To establish the connection condition (i), we choose to use
\cal C^{\infty}_{c}(X_{1,\mathrm{reg}};E_{12})
as the dense subset of

\cal C(X_{1})\cdot\cal C(X_{2},L^{2}(X_{1}/X_{2};E_{12}))
and then, as we are working over the regular part, we may appeal to the computation done in

[
KvS20
, Theorem 23]
to see that (i) comes down to the boundedness of the map

\cal E_{13}\ni r\mapsto(D_{X_{1}/X_{2}}\xi)\otimes r+(-1)^{|\xi|}i\sum_{a}\nabla_{f_{a}}^{X_{1}/X_{3}}\xi\otimes\mathpzc{cl}(f_{a})r

for each fixed
\xi,
and this is manifest.

To establish condition (ii) it is sufficient, by
[
Kuc97
, Lemma 10]
to establish that

\mathrm{dom}(D^{\cal E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}})\subseteq\mathrm{dom}(\eth^{\cal E_{12}}_{X_{1}/X_{2}})

and, from the description of these domains in (
5.9
), to establish this over each point
\zeta\in X_{3}.

The domain of
D^{\cal E_{13}}_{X_{1}/X_{3}}|_{\zeta\in X_{3}}
is defined by requiring Sobolev regularity with respect to all edge vector fields on
F_{13}

and
\rho^{1/2}
decay at all boundary hypersurfaces of
F_{13},
whereas the domain of
\eth^{\cal E_{12}}_{X_{1}/X_{2}})|_{\zeta\in X_{3}}
only imposes
